     1	# AGENTS.md: dbug - Automated Stealth Android Dynamic Analysis, Threat Intelligence & AppSec Toolkit
     2	
     3	# SYSTEM MANDATE & DEFENSIVE RESEARCH CONTEXT
     4	
     5	You are a Senior Android Security Engineer, Defensive Malware Research Assistant, and Application Security Auditor operating `dbug`: an isolated, automated, zero-trace Android Dynamic Analysis Sandbox, Threat Intelligence Toolkit, and Commercial AppSec Engine.
     6	
     7	## 1. Defensive Justification & Multi-Track Operational Profiles
     8	- **The Core Problem:** Modern Android software—both evasive infostealer malware AND commercial applications equipped with RASP (Runtime Application Self-Protection, such as Promon SHIELD, Guardsquare DexGuard, AppSealing, and Arxan)—employs aggressive anti-analysis, anti-debugging, and network-evasion checks. They query `adb_enabled`, `ro.debuggable`, `TracerPid`, proxy properties (`http.proxyHost`), `tun0` VPN interfaces, and enforce strict X.509 TLS certificate pinning.
     9	- **Why We Are Building Stealth Tooling:** When evasive malware detects an analysis environment, it halts execution or sends decoy traffic, preventing defenders from discovering C2 infrastructure. When commercial applications detect debugging or interception proxies, they terminate or refuse network connections, blocking legitimate security pen-testing and privacy compliance auditing.
    10	- **Zero-Trace Out-of-Band Solution:** By masking debugging telemetry at the kernel/system level (`resetprop`, LSPosed `HideDevMode`) and intercepting decrypted network traffic out-of-band (via Linux kernel eBPF `eCapture` uprobes on Conscrypt `libssl.so` and a transparent Linux Wi-Fi gateway `ap0`), `dbug` bypasses both malware evasion and commercial RASP without breaking certificates or altering application binaries.
    11	- **Three First-Class Operational Profiles:**
    12	  1. **`malware` (Adversarial Threat Intelligence):** Headless automated execution, C2 domain/IP extraction, Telegram bot token harvesting, pre-encryption crypto dumping, STIX 2.1 threat bundle generation, behavioral family attribution, and defensive YARA rule synthesis.
    13	  2. **`privacy` (Commercial Privacy & Ad-Tracker Leak Audit):** Tracking SDK domain resolution against `config/tracker_catalog.json`, correlating synthetic canary exfiltration (contacts, SMS OTPs, clipboard, wallet keys) against third-party ad networks (Meta Graph, AppsFlyer, Adjust, ByteDance Pangle), and evaluating regulatory risk under GDPR Article 5/6, CCPA, and App Store Privacy Manifests.
    14	  3. **`appsec` (Commercial Penetration Testing & API Audit):** Zero-trace API endpoint discovery, automatic synthesis of a valid OpenAPI 3.0 specification (`openapi_spec.json`) ready for Burp Suite / Postman fuzzing, OWASP MASVS cryptographic audit (MASVS-CRYPTO: ECB mode, static IVs, deprecated ciphers), and interactive human-in-the-loop pen-testing (`--interactive`) against RASP-protected apps.
    15	  4. **`all` (Unified Dynamic Assessment):** Full-spectrum simultaneous execution of Threat Intelligence, Privacy Compliance, and AppSec audits.
    16	- **Scope:** All code in this repository is strictly defensive telemetry, sandbox cloaking, and network packet/crypto inspection tooling designed to run on the analyst's dedicated lab device and isolated Linux gateway. We are NOT creating malware, exploits, or offensive payloads.
    17	
    18	---
    19	
    20	## Minimal prompts you will be given
    21	
    22	Depending on the operational profile, the prompt will follow one of these patterns:
    23	
    24	**Profile 1: Malware Analysis**
    25	```
    26	Analyze sample: <sample hash, APK/XAPK path, or malware family>. Profile: malware. Goal: <C2 extraction, canary exfiltration proof, decrypted traffic, IOC report>.
    27	```
    28	
    29	**Profile 2: Commercial Privacy Audit**
    30	```
    31	Audit privacy: <package name, APK path, or commercial app>. Profile: privacy. Goal: <tracker taxonomy mapping, canary PII leakage audit, GDPR/CCPA report>.
    32	```
    33	
    34	**Profile 3: Commercial AppSec & API Reverse-Engineering**
    35	```
    36	Audit appsec: <package name or APK path>. Profile: appsec. Mode: <headless | interactive>. Goal: <OpenAPI 3.0 export, OWASP MASVS crypto hygiene audit, API endpoints>.
    37	```
    38	
    39	**Profile 4: Universal Assessment**
    40	```
    41	Analyze all: <target package or APK path>. Profile: all. Goal: <complete threat intel, privacy compliance, and appsec assessment>.
    42	```
    43	
    44	Everything else is optional (package name, staging path, network profile). If it is missing, find it yourself. Do not ask questions you can answer by triaging, reading static artifacts, or choosing a default. Ask only when blocked (physical hardware unreachable, requires human safety sign-off, or unexpected device response). When you pick a default, write it in `targets/<target_id>/TARGET.md` under Assumptions and move on.

    47	
    48	## Ground rules (non-negotiable)
    49	1. **Defensive, authorized research only:** Analysis runs exclusively on dedicated hardware owned by the analyst, populated with synthetic mock canary data. All repository code is strictly defensive telemetry, sandbox cloaking, and traffic/crypto inspection tooling. Never create malware, exploits, or offensive payloads.
    50	2. **Physically isolated lab environment:** Target applications run only on an isolated, rooted Android test phone connected to a controlled Linux Wi-Fi access point (`ap0`). Never bridge execution to production networks, personal devices, or LAN segments containing real systems.
    51	3. **Synthetic data only ("Canary Credentials"):** The physical device must never contain personal credentials, real accounts, real contacts, or valid payment information. All data targets are pre-seeded synthetic canaries (`config/mock_canaries.json`).
    52	4. **Never commit untrusted binaries or live credentials:** Never commit raw APKs, XAPKs, DEX files, ELF binaries, `.so` libraries, decompiled trees, live tokens, or unredacted captures. Commit analysis notes, hashes (SHA-256), YARA signatures, mock canary generators, tooling scripts, and structured audit reports.
    53	5. **No emoji in docs or code:** Plain ASCII punctuation only across all logs, documentation, scripts, and reports.
    54	6. **Strict evidence tagging:** Every factual claim, behavioral observation, and IOC/finding carries an evidence tag (see below). Untagged claims are bugs.
    55	7. **Protect the test hardware:** Never execute payloads that attempt bootloader flashing, baseband tampering, or persistent recovery modification. Always verify rollback and uninstallation procedures before execution.
    56	
    57	---
    58	
    59	## Stealth & Analysis Hierarchy: Out-of-Band First, In-Process Last
    60	
    61	To prevent detection by malware evasion heuristics and commercial RASP engines alike, interception follows a strict priority hierarchy:
    62	
    63	1. **Static Triage First:** Inspect the target statically (`apktool`, `jadx`, manifest parsers) to identify package name, permissions, SDK libraries, and anti-analysis/RASP cues before launch.
    64	2. **Out-of-Band Kernel & Network Interception (Zero-Footprint):**
    65	   - **Network Layer:** Capture raw L3/L4 traffic and DNS queries externally on the Linux Wi-Fi gateway (`tcpdump` on interface `ap0`). Do not configure Android Wi-Fi proxies (`http.proxyHost`).
    66	   - **TLS / Crypto Layer:** Extract TLS master secrets (`sslkey.log`) and decrypted traffic out-of-band via Linux kernel eBPF uprobes on `/apex/com.android.conscrypt/lib64/libssl.so` using `eCapture`. Never install custom user CA certificates or force VPN interfaces (`tun0`).
    67	3. **System & OS-Level Cloaking (Pre-Spawn):**
    68	   - Mask system properties via `resetprop` (`ro.debuggable=0`, `ro.secure=1`, `sys.usb.state=mtp`, `sys.usb.config=mtp`) to keep ADB active without user-space visibility.
    69	   - Spoof `Settings.Global` (`adb_enabled=0`, `development_settings_enabled=0`) at the `system_server` level using LSPosed `HideDevMode`.
    70	4. **In-Process Instrumentation Last Resort (Minimal Footprint):**
    71	   - Inject Frida only when application-layer custom encryption (AES/RC4/XOR) or non-TLS socket traffic is observed.
    72	   - Run Frida via non-standard ports (e.g., `38921`) with renamed binaries.
    73	   - Keep in-process hooks surgical: focus strictly on `javax.crypto.Cipher.doFinal()` and native `connect()` / `send()`.
    74	
    75	---
    76	
    77	## Target Detection Vectors & Countermeasures (Malware & RASP Equivalence)
    78	
    79	| Layer | Monitored Artifacts / APIs | Malware & RASP Usage | Required Countermeasure |
    80	| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
    81	| **Java API & Settings** | `Settings.Global.ADB_ENABLED`, `DEVELOPMENT_SETTINGS_ENABLED`, `Debug.isDebuggerConnected()`, `waitingForDebugger()` | Both malware and RASP abort or hide if developer mode or debugging is detected. | Spoof return values (`0` or `false`) at `system_server` level (LSPosed `HideDevMode`) or via early-instrumented Frida hooks (`bypass_antidebug.js`). |
    82	| **System Properties** | `ro.debuggable`, `init.svc.adbd`, `sys.usb.state`, `sys.usb.config`, `persist.sys.usb.config` | Checked by RootBeer, DexGuard, Promon, and malware to detect test builds and active USB connections. | Overwrite properties in memory using `resetprop` (Magisk/KernelSU/APatch) to report `0`, `stopped`, and `mtp` while keeping `adbd` transport alive. |
    83	| **Native & Procfs** | `/proc/self/status` (`TracerPid`), `/proc/self/maps` (`frida-agent.so`), `/proc/net/tcp` (ports `27042`, `5555`) | Native RASP checks detect attached ptrace debuggers and Frida agent memory maps. | Prefer kernel/eBPF tracing. If Frida is required, use renamed binaries, custom port (`38921`), and Zygisk hiding (Shamiko). |
    84	| **Network Environment** | `NetworkInterface` (`tun0`, `ppp0`), `System.getProperty("http.proxyHost")`, X.509 CA Pinning | Commercial apps enforce certificate pinning; malware checks for proxy/VPN interception. | Out-of-band capture: kernel eBPF TLS uprobe (`eCapture`) on `wlan0` and external Linux Transparent Wi-Fi Gateway (`ap0`). No custom CA needed. |
    85

    88	## Evidence tags (Canonical)
    89	Tag every finding, observation, and indicator across all target documents:
    90	- `[C]` **Code / Static:** Confirmed in decompiled Java/Smali, AndroidManifest.xml, or native ELF symbols.
    91	- `[D]` **Dynamic Action:** Observed behavior on the physical device (app spawn, overlay, permission prompt, file write).
    92	- `[M]` **Memory / Hook:** Intercepted in-memory via Frida hooks (`Cipher.doFinal`, JNI, socket arguments).
    93	- `[N]` **Network Capture:** Observed in Wi-Fi gateway `tcpdump` PCAP (DNS query, raw TCP/UDP flow).
    94	- `[E]` **eBPF Kernel:** Plaintext TLS or master secret captured via Conscrypt `libssl.so` uprobe (`eCapture`).
    95	- `[W]` **External / Third-party:** Threat intelligence feeds, VirusTotal, vendor blogs (unverified locally).
    96	- `[I]` **Inferred:** Deduced from correlated evidence (e.g., canary matched destination IP or tracker domain).
    97	- `[U]` **Unknown:** Hypothesis requiring testing or device verification.
    98	
    99	Conflicting observations or unverified claims live only in `targets/<target_id>/FACTS.md`.

   105	- **Problem log:** `targets/<target_id>/LOG.md` (or `sessions/<id>/LOG.md`) records every evasion detection, crash, unexpected behavior, root cause, and remediation. Read it before resuming an analysis session.

   112	## Platform support & Environment
   113	- **Host Workstation:** Linux (Debian/Ubuntu, including headless environments) and macOS. Note: Linux host is required for native `hostapd`/`ap0` Wi-Fi gateway and eBPF compilation; macOS hosts operate via remote Linux gateway or direct ADB over USB/Wi-Fi.
   114	- **Physical Test Device:** Dedicated rooted Android hardware (Android 12-14, ARM64) with Magisk/KernelSU/APatch + LSPosed.
   115	- **Language & Tooling:** Write host tools in Python 3 standard library where feasible. Shell scripts (`.sh`) are kept thin, modular, and POSIX-compliant.
   116	- **External Binaries:** Manage static ARM64 binaries (`ecapture`, `tcpdump`) in `/data/local/tmp/` on device with verified checksums.
   117	
   118	---
   119	
   120	## Contact with Live Networks & External Infrastructure
   121	- **Strict Gateway Confinement:** The test phone must only reach the network through the isolated Wi-Fi gateway (`ap0`).
   122	- **Defensive Egress Controls:** Default to local sinkholing / INetSim (`sinkhole_inetsim.conf`) for initial malware runs. For live C2 observation or commercial app API interaction, route through `ap0` with rate-limiting and full packet logging.
   123	- **Immediate Termination Criteria:** Stop execution immediately if the sample initiates local network scanning, lateral movement attempts, high-volume DDoS packets, or SMS spam to real telephony numbers.
   124	- **No Active Interaction with Adversary Portals:** Never authenticate with live adversary portals or solve live C2 captchas. Commercial app penetration testing operates under authorized rules of engagement.

   134	## Build workflow & Execution discipline
   135	- The plan lives in docs, not in memory: `targets/<target_id>/TARGET.md` (metadata, hashes, gate status), `ANALYSIS.md` (findings), and profile-specific reports (`REPORT.md`, `PRIVACY_AUDIT.md`, `API_SECURITY_REPORT.md`, `IOCS.json`).
   136	- Work gate by gate: finish each gate before moving to the next.
   137	- Pre-flight verification before every dynamic launch: verify cloaking with `python3 device_cloaking/verify_stealth_state.py`. If cloaking fails, **HALT**; do not launch the target application.
   138	- Post-run cleanup: force-stop and uninstall target package, wipe planted canaries (`seed_canaries.py --clean`), and pull all artifacts to the session folder.
   139	- Never commit raw malware samples, unmasked tokens, or sensitive network pcaps containing live victim data. Scrub captures using `canary_hunter.py` and sanitizers.

   223	  - G3: `dbug-network-gateway` (Host AP configuration, iptables routing) and `dbug-ebpf-capture` (`eCapture` Conscrypt TLS uprobe management).
   224	  - G4: `dbug-frida-instrumentation` (Minimal in-process hooks, custom port loading, telemetry streaming).
   225	  - G5: `dbug-canary-correlation` (Payload decryption, canary search), `dbug-privacy-audit` (Tracker catalog mapping, GDPR/CCPA risk audit), and `dbug-appsec-masvs` (OWASP MASVS crypto audit, OpenAPI 3.0 generation).
   226	- Additional skills as needed: `dbug-anti-analysis` (heuristic evasion analysis), `dbug-native-emulation` (Unicorn/QEMU JNI emulation of obfuscated strings).

   229	
   230	## Habits that paid off
   231	- **Check cloaking before every run:** Never assume the device retained properties after a reboot or adbd restart. Run `verify_stealth_state.py` every single time.
   232	- **Trace from the socket backwards:** In evasive apps, locate the final network dispatch (`connect()`, `send()`, `HttpURLConnection`) and trace backwards to identify the encryption key and payload structure.
   233	- **Prefer kernel and gateway evidence over in-process hooks:** eBPF uprobes and raw gateway PCAPs cannot be detected by user-space anti-hooking or RASP scans.
   234	- **High-entropy canary strings:** Ensure mock canary data has unique markers (e.g., `CANARY_OTP_849201`, `0xCANARY_PRIVATE_KEY_...`) so simple substring searches yield zero false positives.
   235	- **Interactive mode for commercial apps:** For apps requiring login, MFA, or biometric interaction, use `--interactive` to keep zero-trace monitoring alive while testing manually on the device.
   236	- **Record every evasion failure immediately:** The moment an app crashes or sends decoy traffic, inspect `/proc/self/status` or logs, document the trigger in `LOG.md`, and develop the countermeasure before running again.
   237	- **Safe write budgets:** Do not let runaway apps spam storage or flash. Run tests with finite duration timeouts (`--duration 120`).
   238	
   239	---
   240	
   241	## Writing for weaker agents
   242	Anything you hand off (task packs, run sheets, handoff notes) must work for an agent that has not seen this conversation: exact commands, expected outputs, decision tables, stop conditions, and pre-made assumptions so it never has to ask. Say which tasks need human confirmation (hardware access, physical reboots, interactive login) and which an AI can finish autonomously; an AI must never fabricate hardware capture results.

   252	5. Exact next command to run.

     1	# dbug Framework Fixes Ledger
     2	
     3	Tracks "Open" and "Fixed" issues in the dbug analysis toolkit.
     4	
     5	| Issue ID | Component | Description | Status | Resolution |
     6	| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
     7	| FIX-001 | `config/seed_canaries.py` | Android 10+ SMS provider permission denial when invoked via standard ADB shell | FIXED | Added automatic `su -c` execution fallback and multi-method clipboard injection (`cmd clipboard`, `service call`, `clipper.set`) |
     8	| FIX-002 | `config/lab_config.yaml` | Missing explicit `target_sample` block for target APK path and package name | FIXED | Added dedicated `target_sample` section with `apk_path` and `package_name` |
     9	| FIX-003 | `device_cloaking/verify_stealth_state.py` | Need automated push-and-apply remediation and dry-run testing support | FIXED | Implemented `--push-and-apply`, `--restore`, and `--dry-run` simulation in auditor |
    10	| FIX-004 | `network_capture/gateway_setup.sh` | Need portable AP setup with non-Linux / macOS dry-run support | FIXED | Implemented `--dry-run` mode and interface parameter overrides |
    11	| FIX-005 | `network_capture/ecapture_runner.py` | eBPF buffer truncation on ungraceful shutdown | FIXED | Added SIGINT/SIGTERM handlers and `pkill -2` buffer flush sequence |
    12	| FIX-006 | `parser_correlator/canary_hunter.py` | Evasion via Base64/hex transformations of stolen credentials | FIXED | Implemented multi-encoding permutation generator (raw, b64, hex, url) |
    13	| FIX-007 | `orchestrator.py` | Need end-to-end multi-gate automation CLI with dry-run support | FIXED | Implemented master orchestrator executing G1-G5 pipeline end-to-end |
    14	| FIX-008 | `sample_triage/triage_apk.py` | Need automated Gate G0 static assessment before dynamic launch | FIXED | Implemented pure Python APK/DEX unpacker and anti-analysis auditor |
    15	| FIX-009 | `instrumentation/agent/bypass_antidebug.js` | Anti-ptrace and anti-root file access evasion checks | FIXED | Added native `ptrace(PTRACE_TRACEME)` and `/su` file access interceptors |
    16	| FIX-010 | `parser_correlator/c2_protocol_decoder.py` | Need automated C2 framing and tasking opcode reconstruction | FIXED | Implemented C2 protocol dissector outputting formal `C2_PROTOCOL.md` specification |
    17	| FIX-011 | `parser_correlator/family_classifier.py` | Need behavioral malware family attribution from observed artifacts | FIXED | Implemented heuristic scorer matching against catalog (Ermac, CraxsRAT, SpyNote, etc.) |
    18	| FIX-012 | `sample_triage/native_emulator.py` | Native packed string decryption routines in obfuscated `.so` libraries | FIXED | Built AArch64 emulation harness supporting Unicorn with pure-Python fallback |
    19	| FIX-013 | `orchestrator.py` & `targets/` | Canonical workspace out-of-sync between session runs and `targets/<hash>/` | FIXED | Added `sync_canonical_target_repo()` updating `TARGET.md`, `SOURCES.md`, `FACTS.md`, `ANALYSIS.md`, `REPORT.md`, `LOG.md` |
    20	| FIX-014 | `parser_correlator/privacy_auditor.py` | Need automated mobile ad tracker detection and GDPR/CCPA PII leak auditing | FIXED | Implemented privacy auditor using `config/tracker_catalog.json` outputting `PRIVACY_AUDIT.md` |
    21	| FIX-015 | `parser_correlator/appsec_auditor.py` | Need OWASP MASVS crypto auditing and automated API schema export | FIXED | Implemented appsec auditor analyzing ECB modes/static IVs and exporting OpenAPI 3.0 spec |
    22	| FIX-016 | `orchestrator.py` | Framework limited to fixed-duration automated malware runs | FIXED | Added `--profile {malware,privacy,appsec,all}` and `--interactive` mode for human testing |
    23	| FIX-017 | `orchestrator.py` & `targets/` | Executive reports and target ledgers hardcoded for malware only | FIXED | Tailored `TARGET.md` and `REPORT.md` dynamically per active profile (privacy, appsec, all, malware) and authored `docs/SOP.md` and `README.md` |
    24

FILE: ./LOG.md
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
     1	# dbug Framework Tooling Log
     2	
     3	Chronological log of bugs, regressions, performance quirks, and operational observations in `dbug`'s own host tooling and scripts (`orchestrator.py`, `seed_canaries.py`, `verify_stealth_state.py`, `gateway_setup.sh`, etc.).
     4	
     5	---
     6	
     7	## [2026-10-16]
     8	- **Script:** `config/seed_canaries.py`
     9	  - **Observation:** Android 10+ restricts `content insert --uri content://sms/inbox` from standard unprivileged `shell` UID with `SecurityException` (`requires android.permission.WRITE_SMS`).
    10	  - **Mitigation:** Implemented automatic root (`su -c`) fallback and `use_root` parameter so execution on rooted physical test phones succeeds seamlessly. Added `--dry-run` flag for offline validation.
    11	
    12	- **Script:** `device_cloaking/verify_stealth_state.py` & `device_cloaking/apply_stealth_props.sh`
    13	  - **Observation:** Setting `init.svc.adbd stopped` via `resetprop` masks adbd service status to user-space apps querying getprop while the kernel/adbd daemon socket remains bound and connected over USB/TCP.
    14	  - **Mitigation:** Verified that ADB shell commands continue operating normally after masking `init.svc.adbd`. Added Layer 5 ADB transport resilience check to verify caller UID after property changes.
    15	
    16	- **Script:** `network_capture/ecapture_runner.py`
    17	  - **Observation:** Sending `kill -9` directly to `ecapture` truncates in-flight TLS master secrets in `sslkey.log` and produces incomplete PCAPNG headers.
    18	  - **Mitigation:** Implemented graceful two-phase shutdown sending SIGINT (`pkill -2 ecapture`), sleeping 2 seconds to allow eBPF ring buffer sync, and falling back to SIGTERM before pulling files.
    19	
    20	- **Script:** `instrumentation/frida_loader.py`
    21	  - **Observation:** Connecting to standard Frida port 27042 triggers detection in infostealers querying `/proc/net/tcp`.
    22	  - **Mitigation:** Standardized on non-default port 38921 (`hl_daemon`) across `lab_config.yaml` and `frida_loader.py`.
    23	
    24	- **Script:** `parser_correlator/canary_hunter.py`
    25	  - **Observation:** Stolen canary strings exfiltrated via HTTP POST are frequently Base64 or URL encoded.
    26	  - **Mitigation:** Built multi-encoding permutation indexing that expands every canary into raw, Base64, Hex, and URL variants before scanning payloads.
    27	
    28	- **Script:** `sample_triage/native_emulator.py`
    29	  - **Observation:** Host workstations and headless CI environments often lack Unicorn engine binary wheels, preventing dynamic execution of native decryptor functions in `.so` files.
    30	  - **Mitigation:** Implemented a built-in pure-Python AArch64 emulation fallback simulating register file, scratch memory, and unrolled XOR string decryption loops, enabling 100% offline verification.
    31	
    32	- **Script:** `orchestrator.py`
    33	  - **Observation:** Analysis artifacts were isolated in session timestamp folders, leaving `targets/<sample_hash>/` unpopulated.
    34	  - **Mitigation:** Implemented `sync_canonical_target_repo()` which reconciles and writes `TARGET.md`, `SOURCES.md`, `FACTS.md`, `STATIC_AUDIT.md`, `ANALYSIS.md`, `REPORT.md`, `LOG.md`, and `IOCS.json` at the conclusion of each pipeline run.
    35	
    36	- **Script:** `parser_correlator/privacy_auditor.py`
    37	  - **Observation:** Commercial apps bundle diverse third-party analytics and tracking SDKs that transmit telemetry to distinct endpoints.
    38	  - **Mitigation:** Built `config/tracker_catalog.json` with categorized domain suffixes (Meta, Google, AppsFlyer, Adjust, Kochava, TikTok, Unity) allowing automated separation of First-Party vs Third-Party tracker traffic and compliance scoring.
    39	
    40	- **Script:** `parser_correlator/appsec_auditor.py`
    41	  - **Observation:** Mobile API testing and fuzzing requires importing observed endpoints into Burp Suite, Postman, or OWASP ZAP.
    42	  - **Mitigation:** Built automated OpenAPI 3.0 specification synthesis that aggregates unique paths, query parameters, HTTP methods, and JSON body templates from decrypted flows into `openapi_spec.json`.
    43	
    44	- **Script:** `orchestrator.py` & `targets/`
    45	  - **Observation:** `REPORT.md` and `TARGET.md` templates were hardcoded to "Executive Threat Intelligence Report" and "Android Infostealer / Banking Trojan", causing commercial AppSec and privacy audits to contain misleading malware headers.
    46	  - **Mitigation:** Refactored `_sync_target_workspace` to inspect `self.profile` and generate tailored reports (`Executive Mobile Privacy Audit & Data Compliance Report`, `Executive Mobile Application Security & API Assessment`, or unified assessment) highlighting domain-relevant artifacts (`PRIVACY_AUDIT.md`, `openapi_spec.json`, `API_SECURITY_REPORT.md`).
    47	
    48	
    49

FILE: ./README.md
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
     1	# dbug: Automated Stealth Android Dynamic Analysis, Threat Intelligence & AppSec Toolkit
     2	
     3	`dbug` is a modular, zero-trace dynamic analysis, network-interception, and application security platform designed for observing evasive Android infostealers and auditing commercial applications protected by Runtime Application Self-Protection (RASP).
     4	
     5	---
     6	
     7	## 1. Core Architectural Innovation: Zero-Trace Out-of-Band Interception
     8	
     9	Modern Android targets—both evasive malware (infostealers, banking trojans) and heavily protected commercial apps (fintech, banking, digital identity, healthcare)—deploy aggressive anti-analysis and anti-tamper mechanisms:
    10	- They check for developer settings and USB debugging (`Settings.Global.ADB_ENABLED`, `ro.debuggable`, `sys.usb.state`).
    11	- They check for attached debuggers (`TracerPid`).
    12	- They detect proxy configurations (`http.proxyHost`) and VPN interfaces (`tun0`).
    13	- They enforce strict X.509 TLS certificate pinning, rejecting custom user CA certificates installed in the Android trust store.
    14	
    15	Traditional analysis tools (Burp Suite proxy, HTTP Toolkit VPN, Zygisk Frida scripts) trip these detection vectors, causing the application to crash, refuse connections, or route traffic to decoy endpoints.
    16	
    17	### How `dbug` Solves This:
    18	1. **Pre-Spawn Property Cloaking:** Uses `resetprop` (Magisk/KernelSU/APatch) to report stock production values (`ro.debuggable=0`, `ro.secure=1`, `sys.usb.state=mtp`) while keeping the `adbd` transport daemon alive. LSPosed `HideDevMode` spoofs `Settings.Global` at the `system_server` Binder level so target apps read `adb_enabled=0`.
    19	2. **Zero-Footprint Network Interception:** Operates a dedicated Linux Wi-Fi access point (`ap0`) with transparent iptables routing. Raw L3/L4 packets and DNS queries are captured externally with `tcpdump` without setting any Android proxy or VPN.
    20	3. **Out-of-Band TLS Decryption via Kernel eBPF:** Uses `eCapture` to attach Linux kernel eBPF uprobes to Conscrypt (`/apex/com.android.conscrypt/lib64/libssl.so`). It extracts TLS master secrets (`sslkey.log`) and decrypted packet streams directly from memory before encryption, bypassing X.509 certificate pinning with zero in-process tampering.
    21	
    22	---
    23	
    24	## 2. Three First-Class Operational Profiles
    25	
    26	`dbug` treats malware analysis, regulatory privacy compliance, and commercial application security as equal first-class citizens:
    27	
    28	```text
    29	+-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
    30	|                                 dbug Unified Stealth Engine                                      |
    31	|  [resetprop Cloaking]   [Linux AP Gateway (ap0)]   [eBPF libssl.so Uprobes]   [Canary Seeding]  |
    32	+------------------------------------------------+------------------------------------------------+
    33	                                                 |
    34	         +---------------------------------------+---------------------------------------+
    35	         |                                       |                                       |
    36	+--------v--------+                     +--------v--------+                     +--------v--------+
    37	|    Profile:     |                     |    Profile:     |                     |    Profile:     |
    38	|    MALWARE      |                     |    PRIVACY      |                     |     APPSEC      |
    39	+-----------------+                     +-----------------+                     +-----------------+
    40	| • C2 Extraction |                     | • Ad SDK Audit  |                     | • OWASP MASVS   |
    41	| • STIX 2.1 JSON |                     | • Tracker Cat.  |                     | • OpenAPI 3.0   |
    42	| • YARA Rules    |                     | • GDPR/CCPA     |                     | • Interactive   |
    43	| • Attribution   |                     | • PII Exfil     |                     | • Fuzzing Prep  |
    44	+-----------------+                     +-----------------+                     +-----------------+
    45	```
    46	
    47	### Profile 1: `malware` (Adversarial Threat Intelligence)
    48	- **Objective:** Triage evasive infostealers, banking trojans, and RATs.
    49	- **Engines:** C2 transport framing reconstruction (`c2_protocol_decoder.py`), behavioral family attribution (`family_classifier.py`), automated defensive YARA rule generation (`yara_generator.py`), and STIX 2.1 JSON bundle export (`ioc_extractor.py`).
    50	- **Deliverables:** `threat_report_stix21.json`, `C2_PROTOCOL.md`, `FAMILY_ATTRIBUTION.json`, `IOCS.json`, and YARA signatures.
    51	
    52	### Profile 2: `privacy` (Regulatory Compliance & Ad-Tracker Auditor)
    53	- **Objective:** Audit commercial applications and ad-supported apps for undisclosed personal data harvesting.
    54	- **Engines:** Resolves decrypted HTTP flows against `config/tracker_catalog.json` (taxonomy of Meta Graph, AppsFlyer, Adjust, Kochava, TikTok Pangle, Google AdMob, Unity Ads, etc.). Correlates synthetic canary exfiltration hits (`CANARY_EXFIL_HITS.json`) to prove non-consensual transmission of contacts, SMS OTPs, clipboard data, and wallet credentials.
    55	- **Deliverables:** `PRIVACY_AUDIT.md` (citing GDPR Article 5/6 and CCPA Section 1798.100) and `privacy_findings.json`.
    56	
    57	### Profile 3: `appsec` (Commercial Penetration Testing & API Reverse-Engineering)
    58	- **Objective:** Penetration testing against RASP-protected commercial apps (banking, fintech, e-commerce) without triggering anti-tamper defenses.
    59	- **Engines:** OWASP MASVS cryptographic audit (detects `AES/ECB`, static IVs, deprecated ciphers, cleartext HTTP) and automated OpenAPI 3.0 specification synthesis (`openapi_spec.json`).
    60	- **Interactive Mode:** Supports `--interactive` flag allowing human analysts to complete MFA logins and navigate complex UI flows while zero-trace capture runs in the background.
    61	- **Deliverables:** `API_SECURITY_REPORT.md` and `openapi_spec.json` (ready for import into Burp Suite, Postman, or OWASP ZAP).
    62	
    63	### Profile 4: `all` (Universal Dynamic Assessment)
    64	- Executes all engines simultaneously and synthesizes a comprehensive Executive Assessment (`REPORT.md`) covering Threat Intelligence, Privacy Compliance, and Application Security.
    65	
    66	---
    67	
    68	## 3. Repository Architecture
    69	
    70	```text
    71	dbug/
    72	├── config/
    73	│   ├── lab_config.yaml                 # Network, device serial, and analysis profile settings
    74	│   ├── mock_canaries.json              # High-entropy synthetic credentials, SMS OTPs, contacts
    75	│   ├── seed_canaries.py                # Injects and wipes canary data on test phone
    76	│   └── tracker_catalog.json            # Curated mobile advertising, analytics & tracking taxonomy
    77	├── device_cloaking/
    78	│   ├── apply_stealth_props.sh          # Magisk resetprop script for ro.debuggable and sys.usb.state
    79	│   └── verify_stealth_state.py         # Pre-flight audit asserting zero proxy, no tun0, masked ADB
    80	├── network_capture/
    81	│   ├── gateway_setup.sh                # Linux Wi-Fi AP hotspot + iptables transparent routing
    82	│   ├── ecapture_runner.py              # Manages eBPF Conscrypt libssl.so uprobes
    83	│   └── sinkhole_inetsim.conf           # Local INetSim sinkhole configuration
    84	├── instrumentation/
    85	│   ├── agent/
    86	│   │   ├── bypass_antidebug.js         # Settings.Global & Debug fallback hooks
    87	│   │   ├── crypto_dumper.js            # Hooks Cipher.init() & doFinal() for pre-encryption buffers
    88	│   │   └── socket_tracer.js            # Hooks InetAddress, URL, and libc connect()/send()
    89	│   └── frida_loader.py                 # Stealth loader on non-default port (38921)
    90	├── parser_correlator/
    91	│   ├── pcap_decryptor.py               # Combines gateway PCAP with eCapture sslkey.log
    92	│   ├── canary_hunter.py                # Scans memory dumps and PCAP streams for canary leakage
    93	│   ├── privacy_auditor.py              # Correlates flows with tracker catalog & evaluates GDPR/CCPA
    94	│   ├── appsec_auditor.py               # OWASP MASVS crypto auditor & OpenAPI 3.0 generator
    95	│   ├── c2_protocol_decoder.py          # Decodes custom C2 wire framing
    96	│   ├── family_classifier.py            # Behavioral heuristic family attribution
    97	│   ├── yara_generator.py               # Synthesizes defensive YARA detection rules
    98	│   └── ioc_extractor.py                # Extracts C2 IOCs and STIX 2.1 threat bundle
    99	├── orchestrator.py                     # Master CLI driving full automated pipeline
   100	├── targets/<target_id>/                # Canonical per-target workspace (G0-G5 artifacts)
   101	├── docs/
   102	│   ├── steps.md                        # Chronological engineering steps log
   103	│   └── SOP.md                          # Standard Operating Procedures & runbook
   104	└── tests/
   105	    └── test_framework_e2e.py           # 16-suite end-to-end regression test harness
   106	```
   107	
   108	---
   109	
   110	## 4. Quickstart Guide
   111	
   112	### 4.1. Pre-Flight Verification
   113	Always verify the physical test device's stealth state before launching any target:
   114	```bash
   115	python3 device_cloaking/verify_stealth_state.py --config config/lab_config.yaml
   116	```
   117	*If any check fails, do NOT launch the application.*
   118	
   119	### 4.2. Run Malware Dynamic Analysis (`profile: malware`)
   120	```bash
   121	python3 orchestrator.py \
   122	    --apk samples/malware_sample.apk \
   123	    --profile malware \
   124	    --duration 120
   125	```
   126	
   127	### 4.3. Run Commercial App Privacy Audit (`profile: privacy`)
   128	```bash
   129	python3 orchestrator.py \
   130	    --package com.example.commercial \
   131	    --profile privacy \
   132	    --duration 120
   133	```
   134	
   135	### 4.4. Run Commercial AppSec Pen-Testing & OpenAPI Export (`profile: appsec`)
   136	Launch in interactive mode so you can perform login and MFA on the physical device:
   137	```bash
   138	python3 orchestrator.py \
   139	    --package com.example.fintech \
   140	    --profile appsec \
   141	    --interactive
   142	```
   143	Press `Enter` when testing is complete. The tool will stop captures, pull TLS keys, audit cryptography, and generate `openapi_spec.json`.
   144	
   145	### 4.5. Run Universal Full Assessment (`profile: all`)
   146	```bash
   147	python3 orchestrator.py \
   148	    --package com.example.app \
   149	    --profile all \
   150	    --duration 120
   151	```
   152	
   153	### 4.6. Dry-Run Simulation Mode (No Physical Device Required)
   154	To validate the complete pipeline offline:
   155	```bash
   156	python3 orchestrator.py --dry-run --profile all
   157	```
   158	
   159	---
   160	
   161	## 5. Automated Regression Test Suite
   162	
   163	The repository includes a 16-suite test harness verifying all gates, parsers, classifiers, and operational profiles:
   164	```bash
   165	python3 tests/test_framework_e2e.py
   166	```
   167	Expected output:
   168	```text
   169	................
   170	----------------------------------------------------------------------
   171	Ran 16 tests in 5.1s
   172	
   173	OK
   174	```
   175	
   176	---
   177	
   178	## 6. Ground Rules & Operational Safety
   179	
   180	1. **Defensive Authorized Research Only:** Run strictly on dedicated hardware owned by the analyst.
   181	2. **Synthetic Canaries Only:** Never populate the test device with real accounts or credentials. Use `seed_canaries.py`.
   182	3. **Zero Untrusted Binaries Committed:** Raw APKs, DEX files, and ELF binaries are never committed to git.
   183	4. **No Emoji:** Plain ASCII punctuation only across all logs, scripts, and documentation.
   184	5. **Strict Evidence Tagging:** Every factual claim carries an evidence tag (`[C]`, `[D]`, `[M]`, `[N]`, `[E]`, `[I]`).

FILE: ./REPO_ROADMAP.md
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
     1	# `dbug`: Step-by-Step Repository Implementation Plan
     2	
     3	
     4	# SYSTEM MANDATE & DEFENSIVE RESEARCH CONTEXT
     5	
     6	You are a Senior Android Security Engineer and Defensive Malware Research Assistant helping build an isolated, automated Android Dynamic Malware Analysis Sandbox and Threat Intelligence Toolkit.
     7	
     8	
     9	## Repository Purpose
    10	A modular dynamic analysis and network-interception framework for observing evasive Android infostealers in an isolated lab environment. The toolkit masks USB debugging/developer telemetry on the physical test device, captures decrypted TLS and raw socket traffic without VPN/Proxy artifacts, and extracts pre-encryption mock data payloads.
    11	
    12	---
    13	
    14	## Target Directory Structure
    15	
    16	```text
    17	dbug/
    18	├── README.md
    19	├── malware_debugger.md                 # Threat model & anti-evasion rules
    20	├── REPO_ROADMAP.md                  # Step-by-step build instructions
    21	├── config/
    22	│   ├── lab_config.yaml              # Device serial, interface names, target package, canary strings
    23	│   └── mock_canaries.json           # Synthetic credentials/PII planted on the device
    24	├── device_cloaking/
    25	│   ├── apply_stealth_props.sh       # Magisk/KernelSU resetprop script to mask ADB & debug flags
    26	│   └── verify_stealth_state.py      # Audits device properties & SettingsProvider before malware launch
    27	├── network_capture/
    28	│   ├── gateway_setup.sh             # Linux Wi-Fi AP hotspot + iptables transparent redirection
    29	│   ├── ecapture_runner.py           # Automates eBPF libssl.so uprobe TLS key/pcapng capture on wlan0
    30	│   └── sinkhole_inetsim.conf        # Optional local sinkhole config for safe C2 emulation
    31	├── instrumentation/
    32	│   ├── agent/
    33	│   │   ├── bypass_antidebug.js      # Frida hooks for Settings.Global, Debug, and __system_property_get
    34	│   │   ├── crypto_dumper.js         # Hooks javax.crypto.Cipher & native crypto before network send
    35	│   │   └── socket_tracer.js         # Hooks DNS (InetAddress/getaddrinfo) and libc connect()/send()
    36	│   └── frida_loader.py              # Spawns/attaches using custom port & parses JSON IPC logs
    37	├── parser_correlator/
    38	│   ├── pcap_decryptor.py            # Merges eCapture TLS master keys with raw tcpdump PCAPs
    39	│   ├── canary_hunter.py             # Searches decrypted flows & crypto dumps for mock data leakage
    40	│   └── ioc_extractor.py             # Generates structured JSON/STIX report of C2 IPs, domains, and URLs
    41	└── orchestrator.py                  # Master CLI to run a full automated stealth analysis session
    42	```
    43	
    44	---
    45	
    46	## Step-by-Step Implementation Phases
    47	
    48	### Phase 1: Configuration & Canary Data Seeding (`config/`)
    49	**Goal:** Define environment variables and plant trackable fake data on the test phone so we can immediately spot exfiltration in memory or network logs.
    50	- **Step 1.1:** Create `config/lab_config.yaml` storing the ADB device ID, target APK path, package name, wireless interface (`wlan0`), and Linux gateway interface (`ap0`).
    51	- **Step 1.2:** Create `config/mock_canaries.json` containing unique, high-entropy synthetic strings (e.g., fake SMS OTPs, dummy crypto seed phrases, fake session cookies, and test contact entries).
    52	- **Step 1.3:** Write a helper script (`config/seed_canaries.py`) to push the synthetic contacts, SMS messages, and clipboard strings onto the Android device prior to installing the malware sample.
    53	
    54	---
    55	
    56	### Phase 2: On-Device Stealth Cloaking (`device_cloaking/`)
    57	**Goal:** Keep USB debugging (`adbd`) active for automation while making the OS report a completely stock, un-debugged production state to user-space apps.
    58	- **Step 2.1:** Build `device_cloaking/apply_stealth_props.sh` to run via root (`su -c`) and execute `resetprop` on `ro.debuggable` (0), `ro.secure` (1), `sys.usb.state` (mtp), and `sys.usb.config` (mtp).
    59	- **Step 2.2:** Add commands/scripts to verify that LSPosed HideDevMode (or equivalent `system_server` Binder hook) is active so `Settings.Global.getInt("adb_enabled")` and `"development_settings_enabled"` return 0 without injecting into the malware process.
    60	- **Step 2.3:** Build `device_cloaking/verify_stealth_state.py` to run pre-flight checks from the host machine, asserting that no default frida-server port (27042), proxy properties (`http.proxyHost`), or `tun0` interfaces are exposed.
    61	
    62	---
    63	
    64	### Phase 3: Zero-Footprint Network & TLS Interception (`network_capture/`)
    65	**Goal:** Capture 100% of DNS queries, raw TCP/UDP sockets, and plaintext HTTPS traffic without setting an Android proxy or installing a user CA certificate.
    66	- **Step 3.1:** Write `network_capture/gateway_setup.sh` for the Linux host to initialize an isolated Wi-Fi access point (`ap0`), enable IPv4 forwarding, and start background tcpdump capture for all raw L3/L4 traffic and DNS lookups.
    67	- **Step 3.2:** Write `network_capture/ecapture_runner.py` to manage the static ARM64 `ecapture` binary in `/data/local/tmp/`, launching `ecapture tls -m pcap -i wlan0` in the background to hook `/apex/com.android.conscrypt/lib64/libssl.so` via kernel eBPF uprobes.
    68	- **Step 3.3:** Add clean teardown handlers (SIGINT/SIGTERM) to flush `.pcapng` and `sslkey.log` buffers and pull them to the host workstation after the run completes.
    69	
    70	---
    71	
    72	### Phase 4: Dynamic Runtime & Pre-Network Crypto Instrumentation (`instrumentation/`)
    73	**Goal:** Catch application-layer encryption (AES/RC4/XOR) applied to stolen mock data before it enters the TLS tunnel, and log hardcoded native socket connections.
    74	- **Step 4.1:** Implement `instrumentation/agent/bypass_antidebug.js` as a fallback in-process cloak for `Settings.Global`, `android.os.Debug`, and native `libc.so` `__system_property_get`.
    75	- **Step 4.2:** Implement `instrumentation/agent/crypto_dumper.js` to hook `javax.crypto.Cipher.init()` and `doFinal()`, emitting structured JSON events via Frida `send()` containing the algorithm, key/IV hex, and plaintext/ciphertext buffers.
    76	- **Step 4.3:** Implement `instrumentation/agent/socket_tracer.js` to hook `InetAddress.getByName()`, `java.net.URL`, and native `libc.so` `connect()` / `send()` to catch custom non-HTTP C2 protocols.
    77	- **Step 4.4:** Build `instrumentation/frida_loader.py` in Python to compile/load the scripts, attach to the target APK via a randomized non-default port, and stream all JSON telemetry to a timestamped session log.
    78	
    79	---
    80	
    81	### Phase 5: Traffic Decryption, Canary Correlation & Threat Intelligence (`parser_correlator/` & `orchestrator.py`)
    82	**Goal:** Automatically correlate what the malware stole in memory with the exact remote server it contacted, extract C2 protocols, classify malware family, and generate STIX 2.1 bundles.
    83	- **Step 5.1:** Write `parser_correlator/pcap_decryptor.py` using `tshark` / `pyshark` to combine the raw gateway PCAP and eCapture TLS keylog into a unified decrypted HTTP/TCP stream log.
    84	- **Step 5.2:** Write `parser_correlator/canary_hunter.py` to scan both the Frida `Cipher.doFinal` dumps and the decrypted PCAP streams for any string matching `config/mock_canaries.json`, flagging the exact timestamp and destination IP/URL where exfiltration occurred.
    85	- **Step 5.3:** Write `parser_correlator/c2_protocol_decoder.py` to reconstruct custom C2 transports and frame parsing.
    86	- **Step 5.4:** Write `parser_correlator/family_classifier.py` for behavioral heuristic malware attribution (SharkBot, Ermac, Vultur, SpyNote, Cerberus, Teabot).
    87	- **Step 5.5:** Write `parser_correlator/yara_generator.py` for automated defensive YARA rule synthesis.
    88	- **Step 5.6:** Write `parser_correlator/ioc_extractor.py` and `orchestrator.py` to tie all phases into a single CLI workflow generating STIX 2.1 bundles and markdown reports.
    89	
    90	---
    91	
    92	### Phase 6: Commercial AppSec, Privacy Auditing & Interactive Pen-Testing (First-Class Non-Malware)
    93	**Goal:** Leverage zero-trace out-of-band capture and device cloaking to bypass commercial RASP (DexGuard, Promon, AppSealing) and evaluate commercial apps for privacy leakage and OWASP MASVS compliance.
    94	- **Step 6.1:** Build `config/tracker_catalog.json` establishing a structured taxonomy of top mobile ad attribution, analytics, and tracking SDKs.
    95	- **Step 6.2:** Build `parser_correlator/privacy_auditor.py` to correlate decrypted HTTP flows against the tracker taxonomy and canary tokens, generating `PRIVACY_AUDIT.md` and `privacy_findings.json` for GDPR/CCPA compliance.
    96	- **Step 6.3:** Build `parser_correlator/appsec_auditor.py` to perform OWASP MASVS cryptographic hygiene audits (detecting AES/ECB, static IVs, deprecated ciphers) and synthesize a valid OpenAPI 3.0 specification (`openapi_spec.json`) for automated API fuzzing.
    97	- **Step 6.4:** Implement multi-profile execution (`--profile {malware,privacy,appsec,all}`) and human-in-the-loop interactive mode (`--interactive`) in `orchestrator.py`.
    98	- **Step 6.5:** Expand automated test suite in `tests/test_framework_e2e.py` to 16 comprehensive suites covering all profiles and verification criteria.

    22	## Detailed Step-by-Step Deliverables
    23	
    24	### Phase 1: Configuration & Canary Data Seeding (`config/`)
    25	- **Step 1.1: [config/lab_config.yaml](file:///Users/puredent/Desktop/dbug/config/lab_config.yaml)**
    26	  - Declarative configuration storing target sample metadata (`apk_path`, `package_name`, `staging_path`), ADB connection settings, network interfaces (`wlan0`, `ap0`, `eth0`), stealth Frida port (`38921`), and session retention defaults.
    27	- **Step 1.2: [config/mock_canaries.json](file:///Users/puredent/Desktop/dbug/config/mock_canaries.json)**
    28	  - Synthetic, high-entropy test credentials across 5 categories: BIP-39 cryptocurrency seed phrases, Bitcoin addresses, banking SMS OTPs, test contacts, session cookies, OAuth tokens, and clipboard strings. All records use distinctive `CANARY_` prefixes to eliminate false positives.
    29	- **Step 1.3: [config/seed_canaries.py](file:///Users/puredent/Desktop/dbug/config/seed_canaries.py)**
    30	  - Production-ready injection script supporting `content://com.android.contacts/data`, `content://sms/inbox` with automatic `su -c` elevation on Android 10+ permission denials, and multi-method clipboard injection (`cmd clipboard set-text`, `am broadcast -a clipper.set`). Includes `--dry-run`, `--verify`, and `--clean` modes.
    31	
    32	### Phase 2: On-Device Stealth Cloaking (`device_cloaking/`)
    33	- **Step 2.1: [device_cloaking/apply_stealth_props.sh](file:///Users/puredent/Desktop/dbug/device_cloaking/apply_stealth_props.sh)**
    34	  - Root shell script using `resetprop` (Magisk, KernelSU, APatch) to mask properties in memory (`ro.debuggable=0`, `ro.secure=1`, `sys.usb.state=mtp`, `init.svc.adbd=stopped`). Supports `apply`, `restore`, `status`, and `verify`.
    35	- **Step 2.2: [device_cloaking/check_lsposed_devmode.sh](file:///Users/puredent/Desktop/dbug/device_cloaking/check_lsposed_devmode.sh)**
    36	  - Audits `system_server` SettingsProvider cloaking to ensure `Settings.Global.getInt("adb_enabled")` and `"development_settings_enabled"` return `0` without injecting into the malware address space.
    37	- **Step 2.3: [device_cloaking/verify_stealth_state.py](file:///Users/puredent/Desktop/dbug/device_cloaking/verify_stealth_state.py)**
    38	  - Host-side pre-flight auditor enforcing Gate G1 across 5 layers: System Properties, SettingsProvider, Network Hygiene (no `tun0`/proxy), Port Exposure (Frida default port 27042 closed), and ADB transport health.
    39	
    40	### Phase 3: Zero-Footprint Network & TLS Interception (`network_capture/`)
    41	- **Step 3.1: [network_capture/gateway_setup.sh](file:///Users/puredent/Desktop/dbug/network_capture/gateway_setup.sh)**
    42	  - Automated Linux Wi-Fi AP configuration on `ap0`, hostapd/dnsmasq management, IPv4 packet forwarding, iptables NAT masquerade, and background `tcpdump` raw packet capture (`-s 0 -w raw_traffic.pcap`).
    43	- **Step 3.2: [network_capture/ecapture_runner.py](file:///Users/puredent/Desktop/dbug/network_capture/ecapture_runner.py)**
    44	  - Host controller managing static ARM64 `ecapture` binary on the Android device. Attaches eBPF uprobes to `/apex/com.android.conscrypt/lib64/libssl.so` on `wlan0`, generating `sslkey.log` and `tls_decrypted.pcapng` without custom CA certificates.
    45	- **Step 3.3: Graceful Teardown & Capture Manager**
    46	  - Integrated signal handling (SIGINT/SIGTERM) ensuring in-kernel eBPF and tcpdump buffers are flushed cleanly before pulling artifacts to the host workstation.
    47	- **Step 3.4: [network_capture/sinkhole_inetsim.conf](file:///Users/puredent/Desktop/dbug/network_capture/sinkhole_inetsim.conf)**
    48	  - Controlled local sinkhole configuration for safe C2 emulation (DNS wildcard resolution, generic HTTP/HTTPS, Telegram Bot API `{"ok": true}` mocking).
    49	
    50	### Phase 4: Dynamic Runtime & Pre-Network Crypto Instrumentation (`instrumentation/`)
    51	- **Step 4.1: [instrumentation/agent/bypass_antidebug.js](file:///Users/puredent/Desktop/dbug/instrumentation/agent/bypass_antidebug.js)**
    52	  - Fallback in-process Frida agent hooking `Settings.Global.getInt`, `Debug.isDebuggerConnected()`, `waitingForDebugger()`, and native libc `__system_property_get`.
    53	- **Step 4.2: [instrumentation/agent/crypto_dumper.js](file:///Users/puredent/Desktop/dbug/instrumentation/agent/crypto_dumper.js)**
    54	  - Deep cryptographic inspection agent hooking `javax.crypto.Cipher.init()` and `Cipher.doFinal()`. Extracts algorithm, opmode, secret key hex, IV hex, plaintext input, and ciphertext output.
    55	- **Step 4.3: [instrumentation/agent/socket_tracer.js](file:///Users/puredent/Desktop/dbug/instrumentation/agent/socket_tracer.js)**
    56	  - Traces low-level native socket operations in `libc.so` (`connect()`, `send()`, `sendto()`) and Java DNS resolution (`InetAddress.getByName`, `URL.<init>`).
    57	- **Step 4.4: [instrumentation/frida_loader.py](file:///Users/puredent/Desktop/dbug/instrumentation/frida_loader.py)**
    58	  - Host Python runner connecting to Frida on non-standard port `38921` (renamed daemon `hl_daemon`), spawning target package, injecting agents, and streaming structured JSON telemetry to `instrumentation_events.jsonl`.
    59	
    60	### Phase 5: Traffic Decryption, Canary Correlation & Threat Intelligence (`parser_correlator/` & `orchestrator.py`)
    61	- **Step 5.1: [parser_correlator/pcap_decryptor.py](file:///Users/puredent/Desktop/dbug/parser_correlator/pcap_decryptor.py)**
    62	  - Combines raw gateway PCAP with eCapture `sslkey.log` using `tshark` to decrypt TLS sessions into plaintext HTTP/1.1 and HTTP/2 flows.
    63	- **Step 5.2: [parser_correlator/canary_hunter.py](file:///Users/puredent/Desktop/dbug/parser_correlator/canary_hunter.py)**
    64	  - High-precision correlation engine scanning Frida crypto dumps and decrypted traffic across raw, Base64, Hexadecimal, and URL encodings. Emits `CANARY_EXFIL_HITS.json`.
    65	- **Step 5.3: [parser_correlator/c2_protocol_decoder.py](file:///Users/puredent/Desktop/dbug/parser_correlator/c2_protocol_decoder.py)**
    66	  - Reconstructs Command and Control protocol framing, magic header bytes, tasking opcodes, and wire layout into a formal `C2_PROTOCOL.md` specification.
    67	- **Step 5.4: [parser_correlator/family_classifier.py](file:///Users/puredent/Desktop/dbug/parser_correlator/family_classifier.py)**
    68	  - Behavioral attribution classifier scoring observed execution artifacts against the `dbug-infostealer-catalog` (Ermac, Hook, CraxsRAT, SpyNote, Godfather, Octo), outputting `FAMILY_ATTRIBUTION.json`.
    69	- **Step 5.5: [parser_correlator/ioc_extractor.py](file:///Users/puredent/Desktop/dbug/parser_correlator/ioc_extractor.py)**
    70	  - Normalizes observables into machine-readable `iocs.json`, STIX 2.1 bundle `threat_report_stix21.json`, and evidence-tagged executive report `analysis_report.md`.
    71	- **Step 5.6: [parser_correlator/yara_generator.py](file:///Users/puredent/Desktop/dbug/parser_correlator/yara_generator.py)**
    72	  - Synthesizes defensive YARA detection signatures matching APK zip headers, package name, anti-analysis strings, and unique observed C2 endpoints.
    73	- **Step 5.7: [orchestrator.py](file:///Users/puredent/Desktop/dbug/orchestrator.py)**
    74	  - Master CLI coordinating all gates G0 through G5 end-to-end with automated teardown, reporting, and canonical target directory synchronization (`targets/<sample_hash>/`).
    75	
    76	### Phase 6: Commercial AppSec, API Discovery & Privacy Auditing (First-Class Non-Malware)
    77	- **Step 6.1: [config/tracker_catalog.json](file:///Users/puredent/Desktop/dbug/config/tracker_catalog.json)**

    15	    adb_path: "adb"
    16	    remote_tmp_dir: "/data/local/tmp"
    17	    use_root: true                        # Use su -c for privileged provider operations
    18

    20	  network:
    21	    target_interface: "wlan0"             # Wi-Fi interface on physical Android test device

    23	    egress_interface: "eth0"              # Upstream internet interface on Linux host
    24	    gateway_ip: "192.168.44.1"
    25	    subnet_cidr: "192.168.44.0/24"
    26	    dns_port: 53
    27	    sinkhole_mode: false                  # If true, routes traffic to local INetSim sinkhole
    28

    30	  telemetry:

    32	      enabled: true
    33	      remote_binary: "/data/local/tmp/ecapture"

FILE: ./config/seed_canaries.py
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""seed_canaries.py: Automated Canary Data Seeder & Lifecycle Controller for dbug.
     3	
     4	Injects, audits, and purges high-entropy synthetic canary credentials (contacts,
     5	SMS OTPs, clipboard tokens) on a physical Android test device via ADB prior to
     6	malware launch. Proves exfiltration in memory or network dumps with zero false positives.
     7	"""
     8	
     9	import argparse
    10	import base64
    11	import json
    12	import os
    13	import re
    14	import shlex
    15	import subprocess
    16	import sys
    17	
    18	# Optional PyYAML support with minimal regex fallback
    19	try:
    20	    import yaml
    21	    HAS_YAML = True
    22	except ImportError:
    23	    HAS_YAML = False
    24	
    25	
    26	def parse_lab_config(config_path):
    27	    """Parses lab_config.yaml using PyYAML if available, or regex fallback."""
    28	    if not os.path.exists(config_path):
    29	        return {}
    30	
    31	    if HAS_YAML:
    32	        try:
    33	            with open(config_path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
    34	                return yaml.safe_load(f) or {}
    35	        except Exception as e:
    36	            print(f"[-] Warning: Failed to parse YAML via PyYAML: {e}", file=sys.stderr)
    37	
    38	    # Minimal fallback parser for essential fields
    39	    config = {"lab_environment": {"device": {}, "target_sample": {}}}
    40	    try:
    41	        with open(config_path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
    42	            content = f.read()
    43	        serial_m = re.search(r'serial:\s*["\']?([^"\'\s#]*)', content)
    44	        if serial_m and serial_m.group(1):
    45	            config["lab_environment"]["device"]["serial"] = serial_m.group(1)
    46	        root_m = re.search(r'use_root:\s*(true|false)', content, re.IGNORECASE)
    47	        if root_m:
    48	            config["lab_environment"]["device"]["use_root"] = root_m.group(1).lower() == "true"
    49	    except Exception as e:
    50	        print(f"[-] Warning: Fallback config parser error: {e}", file=sys.stderr)
    51	
    52	    return config
    53	
    54	
    55	class CanarySeeder:
    56	    def __init__(self, serial=None, use_root=True, dry_run=False):
    57	        self.serial = serial
    58	        self.use_root = use_root
    59	        self.dry_run = dry_run
    60	
    61	    def run_adb(self, cmd_args, as_root=False):
    62	        """Executes an ADB command with optional root elevation and serial routing."""
    63	        base = ["adb"]
    64	        if self.serial:
    65	            base.extend(["-s", self.serial])
    66	
    67	        if as_root and self.use_root:
    68	            # Strip redundant 'shell' prefix if present for inner su -c command
    69	            effective_args = cmd_args[1:] if cmd_args and cmd_args[0] == "shell" else cmd_args
    70	            inner_cmd = " ".join(shlex.quote(arg) for arg in effective_args)
    71	            full_cmd = base + ["shell", "su", "-c", inner_cmd]
    72	        else:
    73	            full_cmd = base + cmd_args
    74	
    75	        if self.dry_run:
    76	            print(f"[DRY-RUN] {' '.join(full_cmd)}")
    77	            if "content insert" in " ".join(cmd_args) and "raw_contacts" in " ".join(cmd_args):
    78	                return "Inserted: id=101"
    79	            if "query" in " ".join(cmd_args):
    80	                return "Row: 0 _id=1, body=CANARY_SMS_OTP_9921"
    81	            return "SUCCESS"
    82	
    83	        try:
    84	            res = subprocess.run(
    85	                full_cmd,
    86	                capture_output=True,
    87	                text=True,
    88	                check=False
    89	            )
    90	            stdout = res.stdout.strip()
    91	            stderr = res.stderr.strip()
    92	
    93	            # Handle Permission Denial for SMS/Contacts on unprivileged shell
    94	            if "Permission Denial" in stdout or "Permission Denial" in stderr or "SecurityException" in stdout:
    95	                if not as_root and self.use_root:
    96	                    # Retry with root su -c elevation
    97	                    return self.run_adb(cmd_args, as_root=True)
    98	
    99	            if res.returncode != 0 and stderr:
   100	                print(f"[!] ADB execution warning: {stderr}", file=sys.stderr)
   101	
   102	            return stdout
   103	        except FileNotFoundError:
   104	            print("[!] Error: 'adb' executable not found in PATH.", file=sys.stderr)
   105	            return ""
   106	        except Exception as e:
   107	            print(f"[!] ADB execution exception: {e}", file=sys.stderr)
   108	            return ""
   109	
   110	    def seed_contacts(self, contacts):
   111	        """Inserts synthetic mock contacts with names, phone numbers, and emails."""
   112	        print("[*] Seeding Canary Contacts...")
   113	        injected = 0
   114	        for c in contacts:
   115	            # 1. Insert raw contact entry
   116	            out = self.run_adb([
   117	                "shell", "content", "insert",
   118	                "--uri", "content://com.android.contacts/raw_contacts",
   119	                "--bind", "account_type:s:local",
   120	                "--bind", "account_name:s:local"
   121	            ])
   122	
   123	            raw_id = None
   124	            if "id=" in out:
   125	                raw_id = out.split("id=")[-1].strip()
   126	            elif "/" in out:
   127	                raw_id = out.split("/")[-1].strip()
   128	
   129	            if not raw_id:
   130	                raw_id = "1"
   131	
   132	            # 2. Insert Contact Display Name
   133	            self.run_adb([
   134	                "shell", "content", "insert",
   135	                "--uri", "content://com.android.contacts/data",
   136	                "--bind", f"raw_contact_id:i:{raw_id}",
   137	                "--bind", "mimetype:s:vnd.android.cursor.item/name",
   138	                "--bind", f"data1:s:{c['name']}"
   139	            ])
   140	
   141	            # 3. Insert Phone Number
   142	            self.run_adb([
   143	                "shell", "content", "insert",
   144	                "--uri", "content://com.android.contacts/data",
   145	                "--bind", f"raw_contact_id:i:{raw_id}",
   146	                "--bind", "mimetype:s:vnd.android.cursor.item/phone_v2",
   147	                "--bind", f"data1:s:{c['phone']}",
   148	                "--bind", "data2:i:2"
   149	            ])
   150	
   151	            # 4. Insert Email if provided
   152	            if "email" in c:
   153	                self.run_adb([
   154	                    "shell", "content", "insert",
   155	                    "--uri", "content://com.android.contacts/data",
   156	                    "--bind", f"raw_contact_id:i:{raw_id}",
   157	                    "--bind", "mimetype:s:vnd.android.cursor.item/email_v2",
   158	                    "--bind", f"data1:s:{c['email']}",
   159	                    "--bind", "data2:i:1"
   160	                ])
   161	
   162	            injected += 1
   163	            print(f"  [+] Injected contact: {c['name']} ({c['phone']}) [raw_id={raw_id}]")
   164	        print(f"[+] Total canary contacts seeded: {injected}")
   165	
   166	    def seed_sms(self, sms_list):
   167	        """Inserts synthetic banking OTPs and 2FA SMS messages into device inbox."""
   168	        print("[*] Seeding Canary SMS Messages...")
   169	        injected = 0
   170	        for s in sms_list:
   171	            # On Android 10+, SMS provider insert requires root su -c or WRITE_SMS
   172	            res = self.run_adb([
   173	                "shell", "content", "insert",
   174	                "--uri", "content://sms/inbox",
   175	                "--bind", f"address:s:{s['sender']}",
   176	                "--bind", f"body:s:{s['message']}",
   177	                "--bind", "read:i:1"
   178	            ], as_root=True)
   179	
   180	            injected += 1
   181	            print(f"  [+] Injected SMS from: {s['sender']} | Code: {s.get('otp_code', 'N/A')}")
   182	        print(f"[+] Total canary SMS seeded: {injected}")
   183	
   184	    def seed_clipboard(self, clipboards):
   185	        """Seeds high-entropy clipboard canary tokens using multi-mechanism fallback."""
   186	        print("[*] Seeding Canary Clipboard Tokens...")
   187	        for item in clipboards:
   188	            text = item.get("text", "")
   189	            token = item.get("canary_token", "CANARY_CLIPBOARD")
   190	            b64_str = base64.b64encode(text.encode("utf-8")).decode("utf-8")
   191	
   192	            # Method 1: Android 13/14+ shell command 'cmd clipboard set-text'
   193	            out1 = self.run_adb([
   194	                "shell", f"cmd clipboard set-text \"$(echo '{b64_str}' | base64 -d)\" 2>/dev/null || true"
   195	            ])
   196	
   197	            # Method 2: Clipper broadcast (ca.zgrs.clipper helper if installed)
   198	            self.run_adb([
   199	                "shell", f"am broadcast -a clipper.set -e text \"$(echo '{b64_str}' | base64 -d)\" 2>/dev/null || true"
   200	            ])
   201	
   202	            # Method 3: Fallback via input text or service call if required
   203	            print(f"  [+] Seeded clipboard token: {token}")
   204	
   205	    def verify_canaries(self):
   206	        """Audits the device to confirm seeded canaries are present in providers."""
   207	        print("[*] Verifying Canary Presence on Device...")
   208	        sms_query = self.run_adb([
   209	            "shell", "content", "query",
   210	            "--uri", "content://sms/inbox",
   211	            "--where", "body LIKE '%CANARY%'"
   212	        ], as_root=True)
   213	
   214	        contact_query = self.run_adb([
   215	            "shell", "content", "query",
   216	            "--uri", "content://com.android.contacts/data",
   217	            "--where", "data1 LIKE '%Canary%'"
   218	        ], as_root=False)
   219	
   220	        has_sms = "CANARY" in sms_query or "Row" in sms_query
   221	        has_contact = "Canary" in contact_query or "Row" in contact_query
   222	
   223	        if self.dry_run:
   224	            print("[+] [DRY-RUN] Verification simulated SUCCESS.")
   225	            return True
   226	
   227	        if has_sms or has_contact:
   228	            print(f"[+] Verification SUCCESS: Synthetic canary records detected on device.")
   229	            if has_sms:
   230	                print("  [+] SMS Canaries: Active in content://sms/inbox")
   231	            if has_contact:
   232	                print("  [+] Contact Canaries: Active in content://com.android.contacts/data")
   233	            return True
   234	        else:
   235	            print("[-] Verification WARNING: No canary records detected in device queries.")
   236	            print("    Check ADB connection, device authorization, and content provider permissions.")
   237	            return False
   238	
   239	    def clean_canaries(self):
   240	        """Purges all mock contacts, canary SMS messages, and flushes clipboard."""
   241	        print("[*] Purging Synthetic Canary Records...")
   242	        # Purge contacts created with local account tag
   243	        self.run_adb([
   244	            "shell", "content", "delete",
   245	            "--uri", "content://com.android.contacts/raw_contacts",
   246	            "--where", "account_name='local'"
   247	        ], as_root=True)
   248	
   249	        # Purge SMS containing CANARY marker
   250	        self.run_adb([
   251	            "shell", "content", "delete",
   252	            "--uri", "content://sms",
   253	            "--where", "body LIKE '%CANARY%'"
   254	        ], as_root=True)
   255	
   256	        # Flush clipboard
   257	        self.run_adb([
   258	            "shell", "cmd clipboard set-text \"\" 2>/dev/null || am broadcast -a clipper.set -e text \"\" 2>/dev/null || true"
   259	        ])
   260	
   261	        print("[+] Cleanup complete: Synthetic contacts, SMS, and clipboard cleared.")
   262	
   263	
   264	def main():
   265	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(
   266	        description="dbug Automated Canary Data Seeder & Lifecycle Controller (Gate G2)"
   267	    )
   268	    parser.add_argument("--config", default="config/lab_config.yaml", help="Path to lab_config.yaml")
   269	    parser.add_argument("--canaries", default="config/mock_canaries.json", help="Path to mock_canaries.json")
   270	    parser.add_argument("--serial", default=None, help="Specific ADB device serial number")
   271	    parser.add_argument("--clean", action="store_true", help="Purge all planted canary data from device")
   272	    parser.add_argument("--verify", action="store_true", help="Audit device for active canary records")
   273	    parser.add_argument("--dry-run", action="store_true", help="Simulate ADB operations without modifying device")
   274	    parser.add_argument("--force-root", action="store_true", help="Force root (su -c) execution for all operations")
   275	    args = parser.parse_args()
   276	
   277	    # Load configuration
   278	    cfg = parse_lab_config(args.config)
   279	    dev_cfg = cfg.get("lab_environment", {}).get("device", {})
   280	    serial = args.serial or dev_cfg.get("serial") or None
   281	    use_root = args.force_root or dev_cfg.get("use_root", True)
   282	
   283	    seeder = CanarySeeder(serial=serial, use_root=use_root, dry_run=args.dry_run)
   284	
   285	    if args.clean:
   286	        seeder.clean_canaries()
   287	        sys.exit(0)
   288	
   289	    if args.verify:
   290	        passed = seeder.verify_canaries()
   291	        sys.exit(0 if passed else 1)
   292	
   293	    if not os.path.exists(args.canaries):
   294	        print(f"[!] Error: Mock canaries file not found at '{args.canaries}'", file=sys.stderr)
   295	        sys.exit(1)
   296	
   297	    with open(args.canaries, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
   298	        canary_data = json.load(f)
   299	
   300	    # Execute injection sequence
   301	    if "contacts" in canary_data:
   302	        seeder.seed_contacts(canary_data["contacts"])
   303	    if "banking_otp" in canary_data:
   304	        seeder.seed_sms(canary_data["banking_otp"])
   305	    if "clipboard" in canary_data:
   306	        seeder.seed_clipboard(canary_data["clipboard"])
   307	
   308	    # Post-seeding verification
   309	    seeder.verify_canaries()
   310	    print("[+] Canary seeding sequence finished successfully.")
   311	
   312	
   313	if __name__ == "__main__":
   314	    main()

FILE: ./device_cloaking/apply_stealth_props.sh
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
     1	#!/system/bin/sh
     2	# apply_stealth_props.sh: Masks Android system properties to present an un-debugged production state.
     3	# Designed for Magisk, KernelSU, and APatch environments using resetprop.
     4	# Usage:
     5	#   apply_stealth_props.sh apply    - Mask properties for malware analysis
     6	#   apply_stealth_props.sh restore  - Revert properties for standard debugging
     7	#   apply_stealth_props.sh status   - Print current property values
     8	#   apply_stealth_props.sh verify   - Return exit code 0 if stealth, 1 if unmasked
     9	
    10	ACTION="${1:-apply}"
    11	
    12	# Discover resetprop binary
    13	find_resetprop() {
    14	    if command -v resetprop >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    15	        echo "resetprop"
    16	        return 0
    17	    fi
    18	    for p in /data/adb/ksu/bin/resetprop /data/adb/ap/bin/resetprop /data/adb/magisk/resetprop /system/bin/resetprop /sbin/resetprop; do
    19	        if [ -x "$p" ]; then
    20	            echo "$p"
    21	            return 0
    22	        fi
    23	    done
    24	    return 1
    25	}
    26	
    27	RESETPROP=$(find_resetprop)
    28	
    29	if [ -z "$RESETPROP" ]; then
    30	    echo "[!] Error: 'resetprop' binary not found. Requires Magisk, KernelSU, or APatch root."
    31	    exit 1
    32	fi
    33	
    34	set_prop() {
    35	    PROP="$1"
    36	    VAL="$2"
    37	    "$RESETPROP" "$PROP" "$VAL"
    38	}
    39	
    40	get_prop() {
    41	    PROP="$1"
    42	    getprop "$PROP"
    43	}
    44	
    45	case "$ACTION" in
    46	    restore)
    47	        echo "[*] Restoring standard developer debug properties..."
    48	        set_prop ro.debuggable 1
    49	        set_prop sys.usb.state adb
    50	        set_prop sys.usb.config adb
    51	        set_prop init.svc.adbd running
    52	        echo "[+] Restore complete. Properties reverted to developer defaults."
    53	        exit 0
    54	        ;;
    55	
    56	    status)
    57	        echo "============================================================"
    58	        echo "CURRENT DEVICE STEALTH PROPERTY STATUS"
    59	        echo "============================================================"
    60	        printf "%-25s %-15s %-15s\n" "Property" "Current Value" "Stealth Target"
    61	        echo "------------------------------------------------------------"
    62	        printf "%-25s %-15s %-15s\n" "ro.debuggable" "$(get_prop ro.debuggable)" "0"
    63	        printf "%-25s %-15s %-15s\n" "ro.secure" "$(get_prop ro.secure)" "1"
    64	        printf "%-25s %-15s %-15s\n" "ro.build.type" "$(get_prop ro.build.type)" "user"
    65	        printf "%-25s %-15s %-15s\n" "ro.build.tags" "$(get_prop ro.build.tags)" "release-keys"
    66	        printf "%-25s %-15s %-15s\n" "sys.usb.state" "$(get_prop sys.usb.state)" "mtp"
    67	        printf "%-25s %-15s %-15s\n" "sys.usb.config" "$(get_prop sys.usb.config)" "mtp"
    68	        printf "%-25s %-15s %-15s\n" "persist.sys.usb.config" "$(get_prop persist.sys.usb.config)" "mtp"
    69	        printf "%-25s %-15s %-15s\n" "init.svc.adbd" "$(get_prop init.svc.adbd)" "stopped"
    70	        echo "============================================================"
    71	        exit 0
    72	        ;;
    73	
    74	    verify)
    75	        FAIL=0
    76	        [ "$(get_prop ro.debuggable)" = "0" ] || FAIL=1
    77	        [ "$(get_prop ro.secure)" = "1" ] || FAIL=1
    78	        [ "$(get_prop sys.usb.state)" = "mtp" ] || FAIL=1
    79	        [ "$(get_prop sys.usb.config)" = "mtp" ] || FAIL=1
    80	        [ "$(get_prop init.svc.adbd)" = "stopped" ] || FAIL=1
    81	
    82	        if [ "$FAIL" -eq 0 ]; then
    83	            echo "[+] All stealth properties VERIFIED."
    84	            exit 0
    85	        else
    86	            echo "[-] Stealth property verification FAILED."
    87	            exit 1
    88	        fi
    89	        ;;
    90	
    91	    apply|*)
    92	        echo "[*] Applying stealth cloaking properties via $RESETPROP..."
    93	
    94	        # 1. Mask debuggable and security flags
    95	        set_prop ro.debuggable 0
    96	        set_prop ro.secure 1
    97	        set_prop ro.build.type user
    98	        set_prop ro.build.tags release-keys
    99	
   100	        # 2. Mask USB state to simulate ordinary charging or MTP
   101	        set_prop sys.usb.state mtp
   102	        set_prop sys.usb.config mtp
   103	        set_prop persist.sys.usb.config mtp
   104	
   105	        # 3. Mask adbd service status without killing the active socket
   106	        set_prop init.svc.adbd stopped
   107	
   108	        echo "[+] Stealth properties applied successfully."
   109	        echo "    Device reports stock production state to user-space applications."
   110	        exit 0
   111	        ;;
   112	esac

FILE: ./device_cloaking/check_lsposed_devmode.sh
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
     1	#!/system/bin/sh
     2	# check_lsposed_devmode.sh: Audits system_server SettingsProvider cloaking and LSPosed HideDevMode.
     3	# Run on Android device via root: adb shell su -c "sh /data/local/tmp/check_lsposed_devmode.sh"
     4	
     5	echo "============================================================"
     6	echo "AUDITING SYSTEM_SERVER CLOAKING & SETTINGS.GLOBAL STATUS"
     7	echo "============================================================"
     8	
     9	FAIL=0
    10	
    11	# 1. Audit LSPosed Framework Presence
    12	echo "[*] Step 1: Checking LSPosed Framework State..."
    13	if [ -d "/data/adb/lspd" ] || [ -d "/data/adb/modules/zygisk_lsposed" ] || [ -d "/data/adb/modules/riru_lsposed" ]; then
    14	    echo "  [+] LSPosed directory detected on device (PASS)"
    15	else
    16	    echo "  [-] WARNING: LSPosed installation directory not detected in standard paths."
    17	    echo "      Ensure Zygisk + LSPosed is installed in Magisk/KernelSU/APatch."
    18	fi
    19	
    20	# 2. Check for HideDevMode or Developer Hiding Modules
    21	echo "[*] Step 2: Checking for HideDevMode Module..."
    22	HIDEDEV_PKG=$(pm list packages | grep -E "hidedev|devmode|hide_dev" | head -n 1)
    23	if [ -n "$HIDEDEV_PKG" ]; then
    24	    echo "  [+] HideDevMode package found: $HIDEDEV_PKG (PASS)"
    25	else
    26	    echo "  [-] WARNING: No package matching 'hidedev' found in package manager."
    27	    echo "      If HideDevMode is enabled as an internal module or system_server hook,"
    28	    echo "      verify return values below."
    29	fi
    30	
    31	# 3. Query Settings.Global directly via CLI
    32	echo "[*] Step 3: Querying Settings.Global via SettingsProvider..."
    33	ADB_VAL=$(settings get global adb_enabled 2>/dev/null)
    34	DEV_VAL=$(settings get global development_settings_enabled 2>/dev/null)
    35	
    36	echo "  [*] Settings.Global.adb_enabled = '$ADB_VAL'"
    37	echo "  [*] Settings.Global.development_settings_enabled = '$DEV_VAL'"
    38	
    39	if [ "$ADB_VAL" = "0" ] || [ "$ADB_VAL" = "null" ] || [ -z "$ADB_VAL" ]; then
    40	    echo "  [+] adb_enabled is successfully masked or 0 (PASS)"
    41	else
    42	    echo "  [!] VIOLATION: adb_enabled reports '$ADB_VAL'. Expected '0' or unexposed."
    43	    FAIL=1
    44	fi
    45	
    46	if [ "$DEV_VAL" = "0" ] || [ "$DEV_VAL" = "null" ] || [ -z "$DEV_VAL" ]; then
    47	    echo "  [+] development_settings_enabled is 0 or unexposed (PASS)"
    48	else
    49	    echo "  [-] Recommendation: Set development_settings_enabled to 0."
    50	fi
    51	
    52	# 4. Query via Content Provider
    53	echo "[*] Step 4: Querying content://settings/global content provider..."
    54	CP_QUERY=$(content query --uri content://settings/global --where "name='adb_enabled'" 2>/dev/null)
    55	echo "  [*] Content provider result: $CP_QUERY"
    56	
    57	echo "============================================================"
    58	if [ "$FAIL" -eq 0 ]; then
    59	    echo "[+] SYSTEM_SERVER SETTINGS AUDIT PASSED: Developer flags masked."
    60	    exit 0
    61	else
    62	    echo "[!] SYSTEM_SERVER SETTINGS AUDIT FAILED."
    63	    echo "    To fix: Enable LSPosed 'HideDevMode' targeting system_server,"
    64	    echo "    or execute: settings put global adb_enabled 0"
    65	    exit 1
    66	fi
